<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a smooth Riemannian metric on $`S^4\times S^1`$ and a sequence of real Laplace eigenfunctions whose nodal four-volume grows faster than $`\lambda^{1/2+\epsilon_0}`$ for one fixed $`\epsilon_0\gt 0`$. This disproves the smooth upper-bound assertion in Yau's nodal-set conjecture and the proposed bound with an arbitrarily small positive power loss.

- **claim-main-power-law-counterexample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on $S^4\times S^1$, a number $\epsilon_0>0$, and nonzero real smooth functions $u_j$ with positive eigenvalues $\lambda_j$ such that $$-\Delta_g u_j=\lambda_j u_j,\qquad \lambda_j\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad \frac{\mathcal H_g^4(\mathcal Z(u_j))} {\lambda_j^{1/2+\epsilon_0}}\longrightarrow\infty.$$ The metric and $\epsilon_0$ are fixed throughout the sequence.
  - Explanation: On a fixed smooth metric on the product of the four-sphere and a circle, there is a sequence of real eigenfunctions whose nodal four-volume divided by the eigenvalue to the power one half plus a fixed positive exponent diverges. This disproves the smooth upper bound in Yau's conjecture, including the version with an arbitrarily small power loss.
- **claim-step-certificate-gain** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-step)): There exist a $C^1$ neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of the round pair $(g_0^{-1},1)$ among smooth positive pairs, a chart $\Omega$ as above, and a number $\gamma>0$ with the following property. Fix a smooth pair $(A,\rho)\in\mathcal U$, numbers $$\frac14\le l_-<l_+\le\frac12,$$ and suppose that $(A,\rho)=(g_0^{-1},1)$ on $\{t<l_+\}$. For every integer $r\ge0$ and every $\eta>0$, there exist $c>0$ and $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\ge N_0$, with $k$ as in <a href="#eq:round-beam" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:round-beam">[eq:round-beam]</a>, there are a smooth positive pair $(\widehat A,\widehat\rho)\in\mathcal U$ and a nonzero real smooth function $u$ satisfying:* 1. *$\\|(\widehat A-A,\widehat\rho-\rho)\\|_{C^r}<\eta$, and $(\widehat A,\widehat\rho)=(g_0^{-1},1)$ on $\{t<l_-\}$;* 2. *for $\widehat L$ defined by <a href="#eq:weighted-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-operator">[eq:weighted-operator]</a>, $-\widehat Lu=k^2u$, and $k^2$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\widehat L$ on the base $B$;* 3. *$u$ has a sign certificate in $\Omega$ of size at least $c k^{1+\gamma}$.* *The neighborhood $\mathcal U$, chart $\Omega$, and exponent $\gamma$ are common to all stages. The constant $c$ and threshold $N_0$ may depend on the fixed input pair, $l_-,l_+,r,\eta$.
  - Explanation: One inductive step: from a pair that is round near the origin of the base, a small perturbation yields a simple eigenvalue whose eigenfunction carries a sign certificate of size growing like a power of the frequency larger than one, with the exponent, neighborhood and chart common to all stages.
- **claim-seed-envelope** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-seed)): There are a number $a_*>0$, a smooth real function $h_0$ on $B$, a $C^1$ neighborhood $\mathcal G$ of $g_0$ among smooth metrics, and a coordinate chart $\Omega\subset\{t>1/2\}$ with open sets $U_0\Subset V\Subset\Omega$ such that the following assertions hold uniformly for $G\in\mathcal G$:* 1. *$h_0=-a_*t$ on $\{t\le1/2\}$;* 2. *$\max_e J_{h_0}(e)\ge c_0>0$ on $\{t\ge1/4\}$;* 3. *on $U_0$, the Hessian of $h_0$ is positive definite with a uniform positive lower bound, and $0<s_{\min}\le\|\nabla_Gh_0\|_G\le s_{\max}$.* *The maximum in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> is over all admissible tests, including all unit vectors when $\mathrm dh_0=0$.
  - Explanation: Provides the initial envelope function with a positive Hessian test away from the collar and positive definite Hessian with a nonvanishing gradient on a small open set, uniformly over metrics near the round one.
- **claim-repeatable-corrugation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-corrugation)): Under fixed ellipticity and $C^1$ metric bounds in the coordinate patch, there are constants $C_{\mathrm p}^*>0$, $c_{\mathrm p}^*>0$ and $\zeta>0$ with the following property. For any $C_{\mathrm p}\ge C_{\mathrm p}^*$ and $0<c_{\mathrm p}\le c_{\mathrm p}^*$, one may choose a sufficiently small reciprocal integer $\nu>0$, once and for all, such that every prepared cube $P$ at scale $S\le1$ admits a smooth modification $v=h+w$, with $w$ supported in the interior of $P$, satisfying:* 1. *for fixed constants $0<\alpha\le1\le\beta$ and $c>0$, $$\alpha\|\nabla h\|\le\|\nabla v\|\le\beta\|\nabla h\|, \qquad \max_eJ_v(e)\ge c\|\nabla h\|/S \quad\hbox{on }P;$$* 2. *writing $\ell=\nu^2S$, some cubes in the partition of $P$ into cubes of side $\nu\ell$ are prepared for $v$ at scale $\ell$ with the same constants in <a href="#eq:preparation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:preparation">[eq:preparation]</a>, and their union $\mathcal R_P$ satisfies $$\label{eq:local-mass-gain} \int_{\mathcal R_P}\|\nabla v\|\,\mathrm dx \ge(1+\zeta)\int_P\|\nabla h\|\,\mathrm dx;$$* 3. *if $s=\|\nabla h(x_P)\|$ at the center $x_P$ of $P$, then for every integer $j\ge0$, $$\label{eq:corrugation-derivatives} \\|D^jw\\|_{L^\infty(P)}\le C_j s\ell^{1-j}.$$* *All constants are uniform over prepared functions and cubes and over the stated class of metrics. They may depend on the fixed pattern, preparation constants, and final choice of $\nu$. No derivative of the input $h$ beyond its prepared second-derivative bound is used. The pattern and $\zeta$ are chosen before the preparation constants and the final $\nu$; increasing $C_{\mathrm p}$ or decreasing $c_{\mathrm p}$ only requires taking $\nu$ smaller.
  - Explanation: A prepared cube can be modified inside its interior so that the gradient is comparable, a Hessian test persists, a prepared subfamily of child cubes carries strictly more gradient integral than the whole parent, and derivatives of the modification are controlled by the small scale.
- **claim-iterated-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-envelope)): After fixing the data of Lemma <a href="#lem:seed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:seed">4</a> and possibly shrinking $\mathcal G$, there are common constants $\gamma>0$ and $c>0$ with the following property. For every smooth $G\in\mathcal G$ and all sufficiently large $k$, there is a smooth real envelope $h=h_k$ on $B$ such that $h=-a_*t+c_k$ on $\{t\le1/2\}$ and $$\label{eq:envelope-bounds} \begin{gathered} \\|h\\|_{C^j}\le C_j k^{\delta(j+1)} \quad\text{for every fixed integer }j\ge0,\\ \max_{\substack{\|e\|_G=1\\\mathrm dh(e)=0}}J_h(e) \ge c k^{-\delta}\quad\text{on }\{t\ge1/4\}, \qquad \int_V\sqrt{1+\|\nabla_Gh\|_G^2}\,\mathrm dx\ge c k^\gamma. \end{gathered}$$ Here $\delta=10^{-6}$, and the chart $\Omega$ and region $V\Subset\Omega$ are fixed. All corrugations are supported in one fixed initial cube compactly contained in $V$. The constants $c_k$ may be any bounded prescribed real numbers. The exponent $\gamma$ and all construction parameters are independent of derivative orders, subsequent accuracy requirements, and round gluing collars. Constants $C_j$ may depend on the order, the fixed smooth metric and coordinate systems, and the bound for $\|c_k\|$; no uniform bound as $j\to\infty$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: Iterating the corrugation for a number of generations proportional to the logarithm of the frequency gives a smooth envelope with a power-law lower bound on the gradient integral, polynomially controlled derivatives and a Hessian test margin.
- **claim-phase-hessian** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-phase-hessian)): Let $e_1,v$ be orthonormal vectors in $\mathbb R^4$, let $0\le b<1$, and let $H$ be a real symmetric matrix such that $$\|H\|\le k^{4\delta},\qquad J'=b^2H(e_1,e_1)+H(v,v)\ge k^{-2\delta}.$$ For all sufficiently large $k$, there are real symmetric matrices $U,D$ satisfying $$\label{eq:phase-hessian} (U+iD)(b e_1+i v)=0,\qquad H-U\ge k^{-3\delta}I,\qquad \|U+iD\|\le k^{20\delta}.$$ The threshold can be chosen independently of $H,e_1,v,b$ under these hypotheses.
  - Explanation: Given a Hessian with a quantitative positive test, a complex symmetric matrix compatible with the characteristic identity is built that stays a controlled gap below the envelope Hessian, including when the real slope vanishes.
- **claim-polynomial-inverse** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-inverse)): Let $\mathcal P_m$ be the space of complex homogeneous polynomials of degree $m$ on $\mathbb R^4$, equipped with the coefficient norm in a fixed monomial basis. Fix $0<c\le C<\infty$. If $q\in\mathbb C^4$ satisfies $c\le \|q\|\le C$, then $$q\cdot\partial:\mathcal P_{m+1}\longrightarrow\mathcal P_m$$ has a right inverse with norm bounded in terms of $m,c,C$. No lower bound for $\|q\cdot q\|$ is required.
  - Explanation: The directional derivative along a complex vector of bounded Hermitian norm has a right inverse of bounded norm on homogeneous polynomials, with no lower bound on its bilinear square.
- **claim-packet-families** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-packets)): Fix a nonnegative integer $J$ and a real number $M>0$. Finite construction orders can be chosen, before taking $k$ large, so that every center $z$ with $t(z)\ge1/4$ has a family $\mathcal W_z$ of four smooth complex packets with the following properties.* *Each packet is supported in an ordinary coordinate ball about $z$ of radius at most $Ck^{-1/3-100\delta}$. For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, $$\label{eq:packet-derivatives} \|D_x^j w(x)\|\le C_j k^{2j}e^{kh(x)}.$$ For the prescribed orders, $$\label{eq:packet-residual} \|D_x^j(L+k^2)w(x)\|\le C e^{kh(x)}k^{-M}, \qquad 0\le j\le J.$$ The center jets and the nearby jets satisfy $$\label{eq:jet-span} \sigma_{\min}(\mathcal J_z(z))\ge c k^{-3/4},\qquad \sigma_{\min}(\mathcal J_z(x))\ge k^{-1} \quad\text{if }\operatorname{dist}(x,z)\le k^{-10},$$ for sufficiently large $k$. Any fixed smooth reference distance may be used here. The same conclusion holds if the latter radius is multiplied by a fixed constant.* *The exponents in the support, derivative, and jet bounds are independent of $J,M$. Constants and lower frequency thresholds may depend on the chosen finite orders.
  - Explanation: At every center in the relevant region there are four localized complex packets solving the equation to any prescribed finite accuracy, with fixed derivative exponents and quantitative real jet spanning, beneath the exponential of the envelope.
- **claim-packets-wavelength-ball** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scaled-packets)): Fix the finite construction orders in Proposition <a href="#prop:packets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:packets">9</a> and a fixed finite number $K_1\ge0$. Let $\mathcal W$ be the collection of all four packets from each of finitely many centers in $\{t\ge1/4\}$, with $\|\mathcal W\|\le Ck^{K_1}$. Suppose that for every $x\in V$ some center is within $C_0k^{-10}$ of $x$, where $C_0$ is fixed. Set $$R_x=(kQ_x)^{-1},\qquad S_x^2=\sum_{w\in\mathcal W}\|w(x)\|^2,\qquad w_x(Y)=w(x+R_xY),\quad \|Y\|\le2.$$ The coordinate ball in this definition lies in $\Omega$ for large $k$.* *Consider the packets whose supports meet this closed ball. Their centered scaled coordinates $X_z$ are defined on the whole ball. Partition them into $$\mathcal N_x=\{w:\|X_z(x)\|<\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\},\qquad \mathcal T_x=\{w:\|X_z(x)\|\ge\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\}.$$ For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, uniformly in $x\in V$, $$\label{eq:scaled-packet-bounds} \begin{aligned} S_x&\ge c e^{kh(x)},\\ \sup_{\|Y\|\le2}\|D_Y^j w_x(Y)\| &\le C_j\|w(x)\| &&(w\in\mathcal N_x),\\ \sup_{\|Y\|\le2}\|D_Y^j w_x(Y)\| &\le C_j e^{-k^{1/4}}e^{kh(x)} &&(w\in\mathcal T_x). \end{aligned}$$ Every packet in $\mathcal N_x$ has cutoff identically one and nonzero amplitude on the ball. Moreover, $$\label{eq:imaginary-gradient} \left\|\operatorname{Im} \left(\frac{\mathrm d_Yw_x}{w_x}\right)(0)\right\|\ge c \qquad(w\in\mathcal N_x).$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:waves-square-sums} \begin{aligned} \frac1{S_x^2}\sum_{w\in\mathcal W} \sup_{\|Y\|\le2}\|D_Y^jw_x(Y)\|^2&\le C_j,\\ \frac1{S_x^2}\sum_{w\in\mathcal N_x}\|w(x)\|^2&=1-o(1). \end{aligned}$$ The constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed construction orders and on $K_1,C_0$, but not on $k,x$.
  - Explanation: On a ball of the local wavelength, the sum of squares of a polynomial collection of packets is bounded below, packets near the point are comparable to their central value with nonvanishing imaginary gradient, and the remaining packets are exponentially small.
- **claim-first-jet-noncancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-noncancellation)): For every fixed choice of the finite packet orders, the construction above satisfies $$\label{eq:noncancellation} \mathbb P\left(\mathcal B_k\cap\left\{ \inf_{x\in B}\frac{\|u(x)\|+\|\mathrm du(x)\|_{g_0}}{W(x)} \ge k^{-500}\right\}\right)\longrightarrow1.$$ The exponent $500$ is independent of those orders and of the fixed input pair and collar.
  - Explanation: With probability tending to one, the random packet sum plus beam has a first jet bounded below by an inverse power of the frequency relative to the weight everywhere on the base.
- **claim-sign-certificate-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-signs)): For every fixed choice of the packet orders in <a href="#eq:gaussian-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gaussian-sum">[eq:gaussian-sum]</a>, there exist $p_*,c_*>0$, independent of $k$, such that for all sufficiently large allowed frequencies, the probability that $u$ has a sign certificate in $\Omega$ of size at least $c_*k^{1+\gamma}$ is at least $p_*$. The certificate can be chosen from pairs of disks fixed deterministically before sampling the Gaussian coefficients.
  - Explanation: With probability bounded away from zero, the random function has a sign certificate whose size grows like a power of the frequency exceeding one, with disks fixed deterministically before sampling.
- **claim-exact-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-correction)): Let $(A,\rho)$ be a fixed smooth positive pair, let $u$ be real and smooth, and let $W$ be a smooth positive weight. Suppose that $$\|u\|+\|\mathrm du\|_{g_0}\ge k^{-500}W.$$ Fix an integer $R\ge0$. Suppose that $$\|D^qu\|\le C_qk^{2q+41}W\quad(0\le q\le R+2),\qquad \|D^q\mathfrak r\|\le C_qk^{-M}W\quad(0\le q\le R+1),$$ where $\mathfrak r=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\mu(A\,\mathrm du)+k^2\rho u$. There are smooth real corrections $(\delta A,\delta\rho)$ canceling the eigenfunction residual exactly, and $$\label{eq:correction-size} \lVert(\delta A,\delta\rho)\rVert_{C^R} \le C_R k^{-M+2129+1088R}.$$ The corrections vanish on every open set where $\mathfrak r=0$. In particular, choosing $M\ge2131+1088R$ makes them tend to zero in $C^R$ as $k\to\infty$. For sufficiently small corrections the output pair is positive and $u$ is its exact eigenfunction at $k^2$.
  - Explanation: Explicit smooth corrections of the tensor and density cancel the eigenfunction residual exactly, are small in finite norms when the residual is polynomially small, and vanish where the residual vanishes.
- **claim-simplicity-protected-patch** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-simplicity)): Suppose a smooth positive pair has a real eigenfunction $u$ at $k^2=N(N+3)$, with $N>1$. Suppose there is an open region on which the pair equals the round pair and $u=u_*$, containing a point with $0<t<1$ at which $\mathrm du$ and $\mathrm dt$ are independent. Then one can make $k^2$ simple by an arbitrarily small smooth tensor perturbation supported in that region, while preserving $u$ and $\rho$ exactly. The perturbation can be arbitrarily small in any prescribed finite $C^R$ norm.
  - Explanation: A small tensor perturbation supported in a protected round patch makes the eigenvalue simple while leaving the eigenfunction and density unchanged.
- **claim-spectral-persistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-persistence)): Let a smooth positive pair $\mathcal P=(A,\rho)$ have a simple positive base eigenvalue $\lambda$ and a real eigenfunction with a sign certificate $\mathcal C$. If $I\subset(0,\infty)$ is an open interval containing $\lambda$, there are an integer $s\ge1$ and $\varepsilon>0$ such that every smooth positive pair $\mathcal P'$ satisfying $$\lVert\mathcal P'-\mathcal P\rVert_{C^s}\le\varepsilon$$ has a simple eigenvalue in $I$ and a real eigenfunction with the same certificate disks. Thus a closed ball defined by one finite norm can be contained in a persistence neighborhood.
  - Explanation: A simple eigenvalue and a sign certificate of its eigenfunction persist for all pairs close enough in one finite smooth norm, so a closed ball in that norm lies inside the persistence neighborhood.
- **claim-higher-dimensional-products** (construction, [source](#stmt-auto-corollary-1)): For every integer $n>5$ there is a smooth closed connected $n$-dimensional Riemannian manifold and a fixed $\epsilon_0>0$ with the same divergence of nodal measure divided by $\lambda^{1/2+\epsilon_0}$.
  - Explanation: Taking a product with a fixed closed manifold carries the divergence of the normalized nodal measure to every dimension greater than five.

      "exact_statement": "Under fixed ellipticity and $C^1$ metric bounds in the coordinate patch, there are constants $C_{\\mathrm p}^*>0$, $c_{\\mathrm p}^*>0$ and $\\zeta>0$ with the following property. For any $C_{\\mathrm p}\\ge C_{\\mathrm p}^*$ and $0<c_{\\mathrm p}\\le c_{\\mathrm p}^*$, one may choose a sufficiently small reciprocal integer $\\nu>0$, once and for all, such that every prepared cube $P$ at scale $S\\le1$ admits a smooth modification $v=h+w$, with $w$ supported in the interior of $P$, satisfying:*\n\n1.  *for fixed constants $0<\\alpha\\le1\\le\\beta$ and $c>0$, $$\\alpha|\\nabla h|\\le|\\nabla v|\\le\\beta|\\nabla h|,\n      \\qquad\n      \\max_eJ_v(e)\\ge c|\\nabla h|/S\n      \\quad\\hbox{on }P;$$*\n\n\n2.  *writing $\\ell=\\nu^2S$, some cubes in the partition of $P$ into cubes of side $\\nu\\ell$ are prepared for $v$ at scale $\\ell$ with the same constants in <a href=\"#eq:preparation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:preparation\">[eq:preparation]</a>, and their union $\\mathcal R_P$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:local-mass-gain}\n      \\int_{\\mathcal R_P}|\\nabla v|\\,\\mathrm dx\n      \\ge(1+\\zeta)\\int_P|\\nabla h|\\,\\mathrm dx;$$*\n\n\n3.  *if $s=|\\nabla h(x_P)|$ at the center $x_P$ of $P$, then for every integer $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:corrugation-derivatives}\n      \\|D^jw\\|_{L^\\infty(P)}\\le C_j s\\ell^{1-j}.$$*\n\n*All constants are uniform over prepared functions and cubes and over the stated class of metrics. They may depend on the fixed pattern, preparation constants, and final choice of $\\nu$. No derivative of the input $h$ beyond its prepared second-derivative bound is used. The pattern and $\\zeta$ are chosen before the preparation constants and the final $\\nu$; increasing $C_{\\mathrm p}$ or decreasing $c_{\\mathrm p}$ only requires taking $\\nu$ smaller.",
      "explanation": "A prepared cube can be modified inside its interior so that the gradient is comparable, a Hessian test persists, a prepared subfamily of child cubes carries strictly more gradient integral than the whole parent, and derivatives of the modification are controlled by the small scale.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-envelope",
        "exact_quote": "After fixing the data of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:seed\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:seed\">4</a> and possibly shrinking $\\mathcal G$, there are common constants $\\gamma>0$ and $c>0$ with the following property. For every smooth $G\\in\\mathcal G$ and all sufficiently large $k$, there is a smooth real envelope $h=h_k$ on $B$ such that $h=-a_*t+c_k$ on $\\{t\\le1/2\\}$ and $$\\label{eq:envelope-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n  \\|h\\|_{C^j}\\le C_j k^{\\delta(j+1)}\n  \\quad\\text{for every fixed integer }j\\ge0,\\\\\n  \\max_{\\substack{|e|_G=1\\\\\\mathrm dh(e)=0}}J_h(e)\n       \\ge c k^{-\\delta}\\quad\\text{on }\\{t\\ge1/4\\},\n  \\qquad\n  \\int_V\\sqrt{1+|\\nabla_Gh|_G^2}\\,\\mathrm dx\\ge c k^\\gamma.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Here $\\delta=10^{-6}$, and the chart $\\Omega$ and region $V\\Subset\\Omega$ are fixed. All corrugations are supported in one fixed initial cube compactly contained in $V$. The constants $c_k$ may be any bounded prescribed real numbers. The exponent $\\gamma$ and all construction parameters are independent of derivative orders, subsequent accuracy requirements, and round gluing collars. Constants $C_j$ may depend on the order, the fixed smooth metric and coordinate systems, and the bound for $|c_k|$; no uniform bound as $j\\to\\infty$ is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-phase-hessian",
        "exact_quote": "Let $e_1,v$ be orthonormal vectors in $\\mathbb R^4$, let $0\\le b<1$, and let $H$ be a real symmetric matrix such that $$|H|\\le k^{4\\delta},\\qquad\n J'=b^2H(e_1,e_1)+H(v,v)\\ge k^{-2\\delta}.$$ For all sufficiently large $k$, there are real symmetric matrices $U,D$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:phase-hessian}\n (U+iD)(b e_1+i v)=0,\\qquad\n H-U\\ge k^{-3\\delta}I,\\qquad |U+iD|\\le k^{20\\delta}.$$ The threshold can be chosen independently of $H,e_1,v,b$ under these hypotheses.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P_m$ be the space of complex homogeneous polynomials of degree $m$ on $\\mathbb R^4$, equipped with the coefficient norm in a fixed monomial basis. Fix $0<c\\le C<\\infty$. If $q\\in\\mathbb C^4$ satisfies $c\\le |q|\\le C$, then $$q\\cdot\\partial:\\mathcal P_{m+1}\\longrightarrow\\mathcal P_m$$ has a right inverse with norm bounded in terms of $m,c,C$. No lower bound for $|q\\cdot q|$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-packets",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a nonnegative integer $J$ and a real number $M>0$. Finite construction orders can be chosen, before taking $k$ large, so that every center $z$ with $t(z)\\ge1/4$ has a family $\\mathcal W_z$ of four smooth complex packets with the following properties.*\n\n\n\n\n*Each packet is supported in an ordinary coordinate ball about $z$ of radius at most $Ck^{-1/3-100\\delta}$. For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, $$\\label{eq:packet-derivatives}\n |D_x^j w(x)|\\le C_j k^{2j}e^{kh(x)}.$$ For the prescribed orders, $$\\label{eq:packet-residual}\n |D_x^j(L+k^2)w(x)|\\le C e^{kh(x)}k^{-M},\n \\qquad 0\\le j\\le J.$$ The center jets and the nearby jets satisfy $$\\label{eq:jet-span}\n \\sigma_{\\min}(\\mathcal J_z(z))\\ge c k^{-3/4},\\qquad\n \\sigma_{\\min}(\\mathcal J_z(x))\\ge k^{-1}\n \\quad\\text{if }\\operatorname{dist}(x,z)\\le k^{-10},$$ for sufficiently large $k$. Any fixed smooth reference distance may be used here. The same conclusion holds if the latter radius is multiplied by a fixed constant.*\n\n*The exponents in the support, derivative, and jet bounds are independent of $J,M$. Constants and lower frequency thresholds may depend on the chosen finite orders.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-packets",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a nonnegative integer $J$ and a real number $M>0$. For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, $$\\label{eq:packet-derivatives}\n |D_x^j w(x)|\\le C_j k^{2j}e^{kh(x)}.$$ For the prescribed orders, $$\\label{eq:packet-residual}\n |D_x^j(L+k^2)w(x)|\\le C e^{kh(x)}k^{-M},\n \\qquad 0\\le j\\le J.$$ The center jets and the nearby jets satisfy $$\\label{eq:jet-span}\n \\sigma_{\\min}(\\mathcal J_z(z))\\ge c k^{-3/4},\\qquad\n \\sigma_{\\min}(\\mathcal J_z(x))\\ge k^{-1}\n \\quad\\text{if }\\operatorname{dist}(x,z)\\le k^{-10},$$ for sufficiently large $k$. Any fixed smooth reference distance may be used here. The same conclusion holds if the latter radius is multiplied by a fixed constant.*\n\n*The exponents in the support, derivative, and jet bounds are independent of $J,M$. For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, $$\\label{eq:packet-derivatives}\n |D_x^j w(x)|\\le C_j k^{2j}e^{kh(x)}.$$ For the prescribed orders, $$\\label{eq:packet-residual}\n |D_x^j(L+k^2)w(x)|\\le C e^{kh(x)}k^{-M},\n \\qquad 0\\le j\\le J.$$ The center jets and the nearby jets satisfy $$\\label{eq:jet-span}\n \\sigma_{\\min}(\\mathcal J_z(z))\\ge c k^{-3/4},\\qquad\n \\sigma_{\\min}(\\mathcal J_z(x))\\ge k^{-1}\n \\quad\\text{if }\\operatorname{dist}(x,z)\\le k^{-10},$$ for sufficiently large $k$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scaled-packets",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the finite construction orders in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:packets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:packets\">9</a> and a fixed finite number $K_1\\ge0$. Let $\\mathcal W$ be the collection of all four packets from each of finitely many centers in $\\{t\\ge1/4\\}$, with $|\\mathcal W|\\le Ck^{K_1}$. Suppose that for every $x\\in V$ some center is within $C_0k^{-10}$ of $x$, where $C_0$ is fixed. Set $$R_x=(kQ_x)^{-1},\\qquad\n S_x^2=\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal W}|w(x)|^2,\\qquad\n w_x(Y)=w(x+R_xY),\\quad |Y|\\le2.$$ The coordinate ball in this definition lies in $\\Omega$ for large $k$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Consider the packets whose supports meet this closed ball. Their centered scaled coordinates $X_z$ are defined on the whole ball. Partition them into $$\\mathcal N_x=\\{w:|X_z(x)|<\\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\\},\\qquad\n \\mathcal T_x=\\{w:|X_z(x)|\\ge\\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\\}.$$ For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, uniformly in $x\\in V$, $$\\label{eq:scaled-packet-bounds}\n \\begin{aligned}\n S_x&\\ge c e^{kh(x)},\\\\\n \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^j w_x(Y)|\n     &\\le C_j|w(x)| &&(w\\in\\mathcal N_x),\\\\\n \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^j w_x(Y)|\n     &\\le C_j e^{-k^{1/4}}e^{kh(x)}\n                                  &&(w\\in\\mathcal T_x).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Every packet in $\\mathcal N_x$ has cutoff identically one and nonzero amplitude on the ball. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:imaginary-gradient}\n \\left|\\operatorname{Im}\n   \\left(\\frac{\\mathrm d_Yw_x}{w_x}\\right)(0)\\right|\\ge c\n \\qquad(w\\in\\mathcal N_x).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:waves-square-sums}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1{S_x^2}\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal W}\n   \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^jw_x(Y)|^2&\\le C_j,\\\\\n \\frac1{S_x^2}\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal N_x}|w(x)|^2&=1-o(1).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed construction orders and on $K_1,C_0$, but not on $k,x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-scaled-packets",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the finite construction orders in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:packets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:packets\">9</a> and a fixed finite number $K_1\\ge0$. Let $\\mathcal W$ be the collection of all four packets from each of finitely many centers in $\\{t\\ge1/4\\}$, with $|\\mathcal W|\\le Ck^{K_1}$. Suppose that for every $x\\in V$ some center is within $C_0k^{-10}$ of $x$, where $C_0$ is fixed. Set $$R_x=(kQ_x)^{-1},\\qquad\n S_x^2=\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal W}|w(x)|^2,\\qquad\n w_x(Y)=w(x+R_xY),\\quad |Y|\\le2.$$ The coordinate ball in this definition lies in $\\Omega$ for large $k$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Consider the packets whose supports meet this closed ball. Their centered scaled coordinates $X_z$ are defined on the whole ball. Partition them into $$\\mathcal N_x=\\{w:|X_z(x)|<\\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\\},\\qquad\n \\mathcal T_x=\\{w:|X_z(x)|\\ge\\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\\}.$$ For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, uniformly in $x\\in V$, $$\\label{eq:scaled-packet-bounds}\n \\begin{aligned}\n S_x&\\ge c e^{kh(x)},\\\\\n \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^j w_x(Y)|\n     &\\le C_j|w(x)| &&(w\\in\\mathcal N_x),\\\\\n \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^j w_x(Y)|\n     &\\le C_j e^{-k^{1/4}}e^{kh(x)}\n                                  &&(w\\in\\mathcal T_x).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Every packet in $\\mathcal N_x$ has cutoff identically one and nonzero amplitude on the ball. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:imaginary-gradient}\n \\left|\\operatorname{Im}\n   \\left(\\frac{\\mathrm d_Yw_x}{w_x}\\right)(0)\\right|\\ge c\n \\qquad(w\\in\\mathcal N_x).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:waves-square-sums}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1{S_x^2}\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal W}\n   \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^jw_x(Y)|^2&\\le C_j,\\\\\n \\frac1{S_x^2}\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal N_x}|w(x)|^2&=1-o(1).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed construction orders and on $K_1,C_0$, but not on $k,x$.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix the finite construction orders in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:packets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:packets\">9</a> and a fixed finite number $K_1\\ge0$. Let $\\mathcal W$ be the collection of all four packets from each of finitely many centers in $\\{t\\ge1/4\\}$, with $|\\mathcal W|\\le Ck^{K_1}$. Suppose that for every $x\\in V$ some center is within $C_0k^{-10}$ of $x$, where $C_0$ is fixed. Set $$R_x=(kQ_x)^{-1},\\qquad\n S_x^2=\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal W}|w(x)|^2,\\qquad\n w_x(Y)=w(x+R_xY),\\quad |Y|\\le2.$$ The coordinate ball in this definition lies in $\\Omega$ for large $k$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Consider the packets whose supports meet this closed ball. Their centered scaled coordinates $X_z$ are defined on the whole ball. Partition them into $$\\mathcal N_x=\\{w:|X_z(x)|<\\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\\},\\qquad\n \\mathcal T_x=\\{w:|X_z(x)|\\ge\\tfrac12k^{-1/3}\\}.$$ For every fixed nonnegative integer $j$, uniformly in $x\\in V$, $$\\label{eq:scaled-packet-bounds}\n \\begin{aligned}\n S_x&\\ge c e^{kh(x)},\\\\\n \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^j w_x(Y)|\n     &\\le C_j|w(x)| &&(w\\in\\mathcal N_x),\\\\\n \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^j w_x(Y)|\n     &\\le C_j e^{-k^{1/4}}e^{kh(x)}\n                                  &&(w\\in\\mathcal T_x).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Every packet in $\\mathcal N_x$ has cutoff identically one and nonzero amplitude on the ball. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:imaginary-gradient}\n \\left|\\operatorname{Im}\n   \\left(\\frac{\\mathrm d_Yw_x}{w_x}\\right)(0)\\right|\\ge c\n \\qquad(w\\in\\mathcal N_x).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:waves-square-sums}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1{S_x^2}\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal W}\n   \\sup_{|Y|\\le2}|D_Y^jw_x(Y)|^2&\\le C_j,\\\\\n \\frac1{S_x^2}\\sum_{w\\in\\mathcal N_x}|w(x)|^2&=1-o(1).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed construction orders and on $K_1,C_0$, but not on $k,x$.",
      "explanation": "On a ball of the local wavelength, the sum of squares of a polynomial collection of packets is bounded below, packets near the point are comparable to their central value with nonvanishing imaginary gradient, and the remaining packets are exponentially small.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **sign certificate** ([source](#stmt-def-certificate)): A *sign certificate* for a continuous real function $u$ in $\Omega$ is a finite family of pairs $(D_i^-,D_i^+)$ of congruent closed parallel three-dimensional Euclidean disks such that: 1. each pair is the two ends of a straight closed cylinder compactly contained in a Euclidean coordinate ball; 2. these coordinate balls are pairwise disjoint; 3. $u$ has strictly opposite signs on $D_i^-$ and $D_i^+$. The *size* of the certificate is $\sum_i\mathcal H_{\mathrm{Euc}}^3(D_i^+)$.
- **size of a sign certificate** ([source](#stmt-def-certificate)): The *size* of the certificate is $\sum_i\mathcal H_{\mathrm{Euc}}^3(D_i^+)$.
- **positive pair** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *positive pair* consists of a smooth positive definite symmetric contravariant tensor $A$ and a smooth function $\rho>0$.
- **fixed small exponent delta** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We fix once and for all $$\delta=10^{-6}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-delta | fixed margin exponent delta | = | $\delta=10^{-6}$ | universal construction parameter |
| q-certificate-size | sign certificate size in one step | ≥ | $c k^{1+\gamma}$ | all sufficiently large allowed N |
| q-local-mass-gain | gradient integral gain per corrugation | ≥ | $(1+\zeta)\int_P\|\nabla h\|\,\mathrm dx$ | fixed pattern and small reciprocal integer |
| q-envelope-gradient-integral | envelope gradient integral over V | ≥ | $c k^\gamma$ | large k |
| q-envelope-derivatives | C^j norm of the envelope | ≤ | $C_j k^{\delta(j+1)}$ | every fixed integer j |
| q-phase-gap | gap between envelope Hessian and real phase Hessian | ≥ | $k^{-3\delta}I$ | sufficiently large k |
| q-phase-size | size of complex phase Hessian | ≤ | $k^{20\delta}$ | sufficiently large k |
| q-packet-residual | packet residual | ≤ | $C e^{kh(x)}k^{-M}$ | prescribed finite orders |
| q-center-jet-singular-value | least singular value of center jet matrix | ≥ | $c k^{-3/4}$ | sufficiently large k |
| q-packet-scale | scaling radius | = | $r_0=k^{-100\delta}$ | construction |
| q-coefficient-tail | probability that a Gaussian coefficient exceeds k | ≤ | $Ck^{40}e^{-k^2/2}$ | large k |
| q-first-jet-lower | normalized first jet | ≥ | $k^{-500}$ | with probability tending to one |
| q-correction-size | size of tensor and density corrections | ≤ | $C_R k^{-M+2129+1088R}$ | C^R norm |
| q-epsilon-zero | power-law excess exponent | = | $\epsilon_0=\gamma/4$ | fixed |
| q-limit-eigenvalue-interval | limiting eigenvalue | < | $2k_i^2$ | each stage i |
| q-final-nodal-lower | nodal four-volume of the stage eigenfunction | > | $c i k_i^{1+\gamma/2}$ | each i |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-seed** [claim](#stmt-lem-seed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-seed): Explicit seed on the round sphere, local perturbation, persistence under nearby metrics. -> seed envelope with Hessian test
- **step-corrugation** [claim](#stmt-lem-corrugation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-corrugation): Periodic radial wells packed in a parent cube, Hessian test on a two-dimensional family, compactness argument. -> modified function with mass gain
- **step-envelope** [claim](#stmt-prop-envelope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-envelope): Iterate the corrugation over logarithmically many generations; sum derivative increments. -> envelope with power-law gradient integral
- **step-phase-hessian** [claim](#stmt-lem-phase-hessian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-hessian): Explicit matrices in two cases according to the size of the real slope. -> dominated complex Hessian
- **step-poly-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-inverse): Finite geometric series for a nilpotent operator after integrating in one variable. -> bounded right inverse
- **step-packets** [claim](#stmt-prop-packets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-packets): Finite Taylor solutions of eikonal and transport equations, cutoff, localization and jet spanning. -> packet families
- **step-scaled-packets** [claim](#stmt-lem-scaled-packets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scaled-packets): Rescaling to the local wavelength, near/tail split, exponential tail bounds. -> deterministic wavelength-ball estimates
- **step-noncancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-noncancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-noncancellation): Gaussian small-ball estimate on a grid plus interpolation and union bound. -> first jet lower bound with high probability
- **step-signs** [claim](#stmt-lem-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signs): Conditional variance of a directional derivative, Taylor signs, weighted-fraction averaging over a packing. -> large sign certificate with positive probability
- **step-correction** [claim](#stmt-lem-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-correction): Explicit algebraic correction of tensor and density with derivative bookkeeping. -> exact eigenfunction for corrected pair
- **step-simplicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-simplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simplicity): Perturbation tensor annihilating the differential of u, kernel identification, unique continuation, compactness. -> simple eigenvalue
- **step-one-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simplicity-2): Combine sample, exact correction and simplicity perturbation with collar thresholds. -> one-step pair and eigenfunction
- **step-persistence** [claim](#stmt-lem-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-persistence): Min-max convergence, elliptic estimates, compactness and simplicity. -> persistence neighborhood
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-persistence-2): Diagonal iteration with summable tolerances, warped circle realization, Lipschitz projection lower bound. -> fixed metric with diverging ratio
- **step-corollary** [claim](#stmt-auto-corollary-1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-corollary-1): Riemannian product with a fixed closed manifold. -> higher-dimensional examples

          "exact_quote": "For every fixed choice of the finite packet orders, the construction above satisfies $$\\label{eq:noncancellation}\n \\mathbb P\\left(\\mathcal B_k\\cap\\left\\{\n \\inf_{x\\in B}\\frac{|u(x)|+|\\mathrm du(x)|_{g_0}}{W(x)}\n \\ge k^{-500}\\right\\}\\right)\\longrightarrow1.$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-fixed-metric-power-excess**: On one fixed smooth metric on S^4 x S^1 and with one fixed exponent epsilon_0, there is a sequence of real Laplace eigenfunctions with eigenvalues tending to infinity whose nodal four-volume divided by the eigenvalue to the power 1/2 + epsilon_0 diverges.
- **inv-sign-certificate-size**: A sign certificate is a finite family of pairs of congruent parallel disks in disjoint coordinate balls with strictly opposite signs of the function on the two disks of each pair, and its size is the total Euclidean three-measure of the positive-side disks.
- **inv-positive-probability-certificate**: For each fixed choice of packet orders, the random packet sum has a large sign certificate with probability bounded below by a positive constant independent of the frequency, for all sufficiently large allowed frequencies.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-analytic-metrics**: Use the counterexample to doubt the sharp two-sided nodal estimate for real-analytic metrics. -> The construction relies on smooth non-analytic corrugations, so it says nothing about analytic metrics, where the sharp estimate is known.
- **anti-low-dimensions**: Read the theorem as a power-law counterexample in dimensions three or four. -> The construction uses a four-sphere base plus a circle, so it gives nothing about the upper bound in the lower dimensions.
- **anti-lower-bound**: Use the result to dispute the sharp lower bound on nodal measure. -> Only the upper bound is disproved; the lower-bound results remain valid.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main theorem is an existence result: the metric is a limit of an infinite sequence of perturbations, each stage chooses a random Gaussian sample whose success is only asserted with positive probability, and frequency thresholds depend on unspecified finite tolerances (the source states that the constant and threshold may depend on the fixed input pair, l_-, l_+, r, eta). No finite deterministic procedure with checkable outputs is given.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an existence theorem with non-constructive frequency thresholds and constants that depend on finite approximation orders, so there is no finite procedure to turn into a program. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The metric lives on a closed five-dimensional manifold and arises as a limit of infinitely many perturbation stages. It cannot be rendered or simulated as a finite scene. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No explicit procedure or checkable certificate is output. The sign certificates are existential objects inside the proof and are not produced by any stated routine. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument uses probabilistic existence (a Gaussian sample succeeds with positive probability) and a limit of infinitely many stages. It does not give an algorithm with stated cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces analytic objects (positive pairs, envelopes, certificates) and no data structure with operations or space bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no computational kernel to accelerate; the estimates use exponents far too large and thresholds too uncontrolled to define any hardware workload. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardness, keys or adversaries; it is a statement about eigenfunction nodal measure on a Riemannian manifold. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or dataset appears. Gaussian combinations here are a proof device for producing sign changes. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no objective to optimise; it explicitly declines to optimise the exponent, which is chosen with generous margins. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages or fault models; independence among Gaussian coefficients is a probabilistic device, not a protocol. |
| other | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | It is a mathematical limit on spectral geometry: for smooth non-analytic metrics, nodal measure can exceed the square root of the eigenvalue by a fixed power. It is useful as a warning not to assume Yau-type upper bounds in smooth high-dimensional settings. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-smooth-nonanalytic** (hypothesis): The counterexample concerns smooth metrics only. The sharp two-sided nodal estimate for real-analytic metrics is not contradicted, and the lower-bound results remain compatible with the theorem. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem to doubt the nodal estimate for real-analytic metrics', 'Using the theorem to dispute the sharp lower bound on nodal measure']
- **limit-dimension-five** (edge_case): The construction is carried out on the product of the four-sphere and a circle, and by products in higher dimensions. It makes no assertion about the upper bound in dimensions three or four. Blocked use: ['Citing the theorem as a power-law counterexample in dimension three or four', 'Claiming a fixed positive power-law excess below dimension five']
- **limit-finite-orders-thresholds** (hypothesis): Constants and frequency thresholds may depend on the fixed input pair, the collar, the derivative order and the tolerance. Only the exponent gamma, the chart and the neighborhood are common to all stages, so no uniform frequency threshold is claimed. Blocked use: ['Reading the step proposition as giving an explicit frequency threshold', 'Treating the constants as effective or uniform in the tolerances']
- **limit-probabilistic-existence** (nonconstructive): The sign certificate is obtained from a Gaussian random sample that succeeds only with a positive probability bounded below, so a good sample is shown to exist rather than produced by a stated procedure. Blocked use: ['Treating the sampling step as an algorithm with a runtime guarantee', 'Using the sample as a deterministic construction of the eigenfunction']
- **limit-large-exponents** (approximation): The exponents in the first-jet and correction estimates are large and not optimized, and the construction explicitly declines to optimize the final gain. Blocked use: ['Using the exponents as tuned parameters in a numerical scheme', 'Reading the positive power gain as a sharp or best possible exponent']
- **limit-infinite-diagonal-limit** (formal_scope): The final smooth metric is the limit of infinitely many perturbation stages with summable tolerances, and the eigenfunctions are realized for the weighted operator that a warped circle turns into the ordinary Laplace-Beltrami operator. Blocked use: ['Presenting the metric as explicitly given by a closed formula', 'Applying the construction to a finite truncation of the iteration']

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-signs",
          "exact_quote": "stmt-lem-signs) | lemma | `lem:signs` | 12 | For every fixed choice of the packet orders in [eq:gaussian-sum], there exist $p_*,c_*>0$, independent of $k$, such that for all sufficiently large allowed frequencies, the probability that $u$ has a |\n| [stmt-lem-correction](#stmt-lem-correction) | lemma | `lem:correction` | 13 | Let $(A,\\rho)$ be a fixed smooth positive pair, let $u$ be real and smooth, and let $W$ be a smooth positive weight. |\n| [stmt-lem-simplicity](#stmt-lem-simplicity) | lemma | `lem:simplicity` | 14 | Suppose a smooth positive pair has a real eigenfunction $u$ at $k^2=N(N+3)$, with $N>1$. |\n| [stmt-lem-persistence](#stmt-lem-persistence) | lemma | `lem:persistence` | 15 | Let a smooth positive pair $\\mathcal P=(A,\\rho)$ have a simple positive base eigenvalue $\\lambda$ and a real eigenfunction with a sign certificate $\\mathcal C$. |\n| [stmt-auto-corollary-1](#stmt-auto-corollary-1) | corollary | - | 16 | For every integer $n>5$ there is a smooth closed connected $n$-dimensional Riemannian manifold and a fixed $\\epsilon_0>0$ with the same divergence of nodal measure divided by |\n\n\n### Introduction\n\nLet $(M^n,g)$ be a smooth closed connected Riemannian manifold. We use $\\Delta_g=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g\\nabla_g$ and write $$-\\Delta_g u=\\lambda u,\\qquad\n \\mathcal Z(u)=\\{x\\in M:u(x)=0\\}.$$ Yau posed the nodal-size question in his problem collection (Yau 1982) [[Yau1982](#ref-Yau1982)]. In its smooth closed-manifold formulation, Yau’s nodal-set conjecture predicts that the $(n-1)$-dimensional Hausdorff measure of $\\mathcal Z(u)$ is bounded above and below by positive constant multiples of $\\sqrt\\lambda$, with the constants depending only on the fixed manifold and metric (Donnelly and Fefferman 1988, 162) [[DonnellyFefferman1988](#ref-DonnellyFefferman1988)]. The upper assertion is required to hold for every nonzero real eigenfunction, including all eigenfunctions in a multiple eigenspace.\n\n\nWe disprove that upper assertion in the smooth category. In fact, the assertion that one has an upper bound with every positive power loss, $$\\label{eq:near-sharp-target}\n \\mathcal H_g^{n-1}(\\mathcal Z(u))\n \\le C(M,g,\\epsilon)\\lambda^{1/2+\\epsilon}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }\\epsilon>0,$$ fails.\n\n\n<div id=\"thm:main\" class=\"theorem\">\n\n**Theorem 1**. *There exist a smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on $S^4\\times S^1$, a number $\\epsilon_0>0$, and nonzero real smooth functions $u_j$ with positive eigenvalues $\\lambda_j$ such that $$-\\Delta_g u_j=\\lambda_j u_j,\\qquad\n \\lambda_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n \\frac{\\mathcal H_g^4(\\mathcal Z(u_j))}\n      {\\lambda_j^{1/2+\\epsilon_0}}\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ The metric and $\\epsilon_0$ are fixed throughout the sequence.*\n\n</div>\n\nIn particular, <a href=\"#eq:near-sharp-target\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:near-sharp-target\">[eq:near-sharp-target]</a> fails for every $0<\\epsilon\\le\\epsilon_0$ for the metric of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.\n\nThe theorem concerns ordinary Laplace–Beltrami eigenfunctions on one closed connected manifold. Taking a product with a fixed closed manifold gives the same conclusion in each dimension greater than five. The construction makes no assertion about the corresponding upper bound in dimensions three or four.\n\nIn dimensions three and four, a companion paper constructs fixed smooth metrics and real eigenfunction sequences with $\\lambda\\to\\infty$ for which the nodal measure divided by $\\sqrt\\lambda$ tends to infinity, without asserting a fixed positive power-law excess (OpenAI 2026, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) [[OpenAINodalQualitative2026](#ref-OpenAINodalQualitative2026)].\n\n\n###### Background.\n\nDonnelly and Fefferman proved the sharp two-sided nodal estimate for real-analytic metrics (Donnelly and Fefferman 1988, Theorem 1.2) [[DonnellyFefferman1988](#ref-DonnellyFefferman1988)]. For smooth metrics, Logunov proved the sharp lower bound (Logunov 2018b, Theorem 1.1) [[LogunovLower2018](#ref-LogunovLower2018)] and, in dimensions at least three, a polynomial upper bound with a dimension-dependent exponent (Logunov 2018a, Theorem 6.2) [[LogunovUpper2018](#ref-LogunovUpper2018)]. Regularity assumptions between analyticity and smoothness also yield stronger upper estimates: Hezari proved a $\\lambda^{s/2}$ bound in Gevrey class $s\\ge1$, and a $\\sqrt\\lambda\\log(\\lambda+e)$ bound for a specified quasianalytic class (Hezari 2023) [[Hezari2023](#ref-Hezari2023)]. Those hypotheses do not cover arbitrary smooth metrics. Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> disproves the smooth upper-bound part of Yau’s conjecture and even the relaxed estimate <a href=\"#eq:near-sharp-target\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:near-sharp-target\">[eq:near-sharp-target]</a>; the lower-bound results remain compatible with it.\n\nThere is a complementary tradition of prescribing nodal geometry. In dimensions at least three, Enciso and Peralta-Salas realize a smooth closed connected orientable separating hypersurface as the nodal set of a first nonconstant eigenfunction by choosing a metric (Enciso and Peralta-Salas 2015) [[EncisoPeraltaSalas2015](#ref-EncisoPeraltaSalas2015)]. At high energy, Enciso, Peralta-Salas, and Torres de Lizaur realize prescribed stable nodal components, up to ambient diffeomorphism, on round spheres and flat tori, with a localization restriction in the torus case (Enciso, Peralta-Salas, and Torres de Lizaur 2021) [[EncisoPeraltaSalasTorres2018](#ref-EncisoPeraltaSalasTorres2018)]. Their construction already gives arbitrarily high frequencies on fixed analytic metrics. The distinction here is power-law excess in total nodal measure, which is not furnished by those prescription theorems.\n\n\n###### The quantitative mechanism.\n\nWe first work with a symmetric elliptic operator on the four-sphere whose positive principal tensor and positive density can be varied independently. A local corrugation increases the integral of the gradient of a logarithmic envelope. More specifically, the part of a parent cube prepared for another corrugation has more new gradient integral than the entire old parent cube. Repetition at a fixed scale ratio therefore gives a multiplicative gain. The derivatives of order $r$ grow at most exponentially in $(r+1)$ times the number of generations. Let $k$ denote the construction frequency, with target eigenvalue $k^2$. Taking a fixed small multiple of $\\log k$ generations produces a smooth real envelope $h=h_k$ on the base and a fixed coordinate region $V$ such that $$\\int_V\\sqrt{1+|\\nabla h|^2}\\,\\mathrm dx\\ge c k^\\gamma$$ for one fixed $\\gamma>0$, while keeping every fixed derivative of $h$ within a controlled power of $k$.\n\nFinite Taylor solutions of the complex eikonal and transport equations then give localized packets beneath $e^{kh}$. They achieve any prescribed finite residual accuracy while preserving fixed derivative exponents. A real Gaussian combination has a quantitatively nonvanishing first jet everywhere and opposite signs on many pairs of small disks. The first property permits an explicit simultaneous correction of the principal tensor and density to make the function an exact eigenfunction. The second gives stable lower bounds for nodal measure without a regularity assumption on the zero set.\n\nThe distinction between constants and exponents is essential here. At a given stage, constants and frequency thresholds may depend arbitrarily badly on the required finite coefficient accuracy. The exponent $\\gamma$ does not; nor do the chart or coefficient neighborhood. The positive constant in the nodal lower bound and the frequency threshold may depend on the fixed input pair, collar, finite norm, and tolerance. Consequently the construction can meet every finite collection of earlier spectral-persistence tolerances and still retain a fixed power gain. A summable iteration gives one smooth limiting pair. Adding a warped circle realizes its weighted operator as an ordinary Laplace–Beltrami operator.\n\nThese steps also give reusable local facts: a corrugation with a repeatable integral gain, a phase-Hessian construction valid at zero real slope, a polynomial first-jet noncancellation argument, and an exact tensor–density correction. Each is proved below. In particular, the argument does not appeal to a qualitative existence theorem with an uncontrolled frequency threshold.\n\n\n###### Organization.\n\nSection <a href=\"#sec:framework\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:framework\">2</a> states the quantitative one-step construction and fixes the geometric notation. Section <a href=\"#sec:envelopes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:envelopes\">3</a> proves the envelope estimates. Section <a href=\"#sec:waves\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:waves\">4</a> constructs and estimates the local packets. Section <a href=\"#sec:global\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:global\">5</a> produces exact eigenfunctions, proves the one-step construction, and passes to the fixed metric in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.\n\n\n### Framework and the quantitative step\n\n\n#### The weighted base operator\n\n\nLet $B=S^4\\subset\\mathbb R^5$, with round metric $g_0$ and round Riemannian density $\\mu$. Write a point of $B$ as $(x_1,x_2,y)$, where $y\\in\\mathbb R^3$, and set $t=|y|^2$. A *positive pair* consists of a smooth positive definite symmetric contravariant tensor $A$ and a smooth function $\\rho>0$. Define a metric $G$ and a differential operator $L$ by $$\\label{eq:weighted-operator}\n G^{-1}=\\rho^{-1}A,\\qquad\n Lf=\\rho^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\mu(A\\,\\mathrm df).$$ Thus $-L$ is symmetric and nonnegative in $L^2(B,\\rho\\,\\mathrm d\\mu)$, with quadratic form $$\\mathfrak a(f,f)=\\int_B A(\\mathrm df,\\mathrm df)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu .$$ The tensor and density will be changed independently. Their conversion to an ordinary metric occurs only at the end of the proof.\n\nFix a finite smooth atlas with compact coordinate subpatches covering $B$; all $C^r$ norms of functions and tensors may be taken in that atlas. For a pair, the norm means the sum of the two component norms. Equivalent fixed choices change constants but do not change the assertions. All pairs used below lie in one sufficiently small $C^1$ neighborhood of $(g_0^{-1},1)$, within the space of smooth positive pairs. Accordingly $G$ and $G^{-1}$ have common $C^1$ bounds and common ellipticity constants. Higher derivative bounds need only be finite for each fixed input pair.\n\n\nGradients, Hessians, lengths, and orthogonality in envelope and phase estimates refer to $G$ unless another metric is displayed. Integration in the elliptic operator refers to $\\mu$; integration in a coordinate corrugation chart refers to Lebesgue measure $\\mathrm dx$. For a real smooth function $h$, set $$\\label{eq:hessian-test}\n \\begin{split}\n p&=\\nabla_G h,\\qquad Q=(1+|p|_G^2)^{1/2},\\\\\n J_h(e)&=(\\nabla_G^2h)(p/Q,p/Q)+(\\nabla_G^2h)(e,e),\n \\qquad |e|_G=1,\\quad \\mathrm dh(e)=0 .\n \\end{split}$$ At $p=0$ every unit vector is an admissible test. The positivity conditions imposed below on $J_h$ supply a gap between the envelope Hessian and the real part of a complex phase Hessian. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:phase-hessian\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:phase-hessian\">7</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:packets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:packets\">9</a> use this gap in the construction and localization of the packets. Complex inner products and tensor evaluations in the phase equations are extended bilinearly; estimates of the size of a complex vector or matrix use the ordinary Hermitian norm.\n\nWe fix once and for all $$\\delta=10^{-6}.$$ The numerical exponents below leave generous margins; we do not optimize the final positive gain. Positive constants written $c,C,C_r$ may change between occurrences. Any additional dependencies are specified where their uniformity matters. In particular, a finite approximation order is fixed before a frequency tends to infinity.\n\n\n#### A round region and sign certificates\n\n\nFor an integer $N>1$, let $$\\label{eq:round-beam}\n k=\\sqrt{N(N+3)},\\qquad\n u_*=\\operatorname{Re}(x_1+i x_2)^N .$$ The Euclidean polynomial in <a href=\"#eq:round-beam\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:round-beam\">[eq:round-beam]</a> is homogeneous of degree $N$ and harmonic in $\\mathbb R^5$. The polar-coordinate formula $$\\Delta_{\\mathbb R^5}\n =\\partial_r^2+\\frac4r\\partial_r+\\frac1{r^2}\\Delta_{g_0}$$ therefore gives $-\\Delta_{g_0}u_*=k^2u_*$ on $B$. Where $t<1$, the angular coordinate $\\vartheta$ in the $(x_1,x_2)$-plane also gives $$u_*=(1-t)^{N/2}\\cos(N\\vartheta).$$ The round beam will be retained in part of a region where the input pair is round. This region serves two purposes: it supplies an exact solution to which the packets can be glued, and it leaves a patch where a later tensor perturbation can make the eigenvalue simple while preserving that solution. The construction uses both properties before passing to the limiting operator.\n\nThe envelope construction fixes a coordinate chart $\\Omega\\subset\\{t>1/2\\}$ and an open region $V$ whose closure is compact in $\\Omega$. The initial corrugation cube is compactly contained in $V$. Euclidean balls centered in $V$ with radii tending uniformly to zero are consequently contained in $\\Omega$.\n\n\n<div id=\"def:certificate\" class=\"definition\">\n\n**Definition 2**. A *sign certificate* for a continuous real function $u$ in $\\Omega$ is a finite family of pairs $(D_i^-,D_i^+)$ of congruent closed parallel three-dimensional Euclidean disks such that:\n\n1.  each pair is the two ends of a straight closed cylinder compactly contained in a Euclidean coordinate ball;\n\n2.  these coordinate balls are pairwise disjoint;\n\n3.  $u$ has strictly opposite signs on $D_i^-$ and $D_i^+$.\n\nThe *size* of the certificate is $\\sum_i\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm{Euc}}^3(D_i^+)$.\n\n</div>\n\nThe signs in Definition <a href=\"#def:certificate\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:certificate\">2</a> can be reversed on each pair. Since the disks are compact and finite in number, a certificate persists under sufficiently small uniform perturbations of $u$. It is also preserved by multiplying $u$ by any nonzero real constant. Every segment joining corresponding points of $D_i^-$ and $D_i^+$ contains a zero. This elementary projection property will provide the Hausdorff-measure bound for the final metric.\n\n\n#### The one-step construction\n\n\n<div id=\"prop:step\" class=\"proposition\">\n\n**Proposition 3**. *There exist a $C^1$ neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ of the round pair $(g_0^{-1},1)$ among smooth positive pairs, a chart $\\Omega$ as above, and a number $\\gamma>0$ with the following property. Fix a smooth pair $(A,\\rho)\\in\\mathcal U$, numbers $$\\frac14\\le l_-<l_+\\le\\frac12,$$ and suppose that $(A,\\rho)=(g_0^{-1},1)$ on $\\{t<l_+\\}$. For every integer $r\\ge0$ and every $\\eta>0$, there exist $c>0$ and $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\\ge N_0$, with $k$ as in <a href=\"#eq:round-beam\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:round-beam\">[eq:round-beam]</a>, there are a smooth positive pair $(\\widehat A,\\widehat\\rho)\\in\\mathcal U$ and a nonzero real smooth function $u$ satisfying:*\n\n1.  *$\\|(\\widehat A-A,\\widehat\\rho-\\rho)\\|_{C^r}<\\eta$, and $(\\widehat A,\\widehat\\rho)=(g_0^{-1},1)$ on $\\{t<l_-\\}$;*\n\n2.  *for $\\widehat L$ defined by <a href=\"#eq:weighted-operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-operator\">[eq:weighted-operator]</a>, $-\\widehat Lu=k^2u$, and $k^2$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\\widehat L$ on the base $B$;*\n\n3.  *$u$ has a sign certificate in $\\Omega$ of size at least $c k^{1+\\gamma}$.*\n\n*The neighborhood $\\mathcal U$, chart $\\Omega$, and exponent $\\gamma$ are common to all stages. The constant $c$ and threshold $N_0$ may depend on the fixed input pair, $l_-,l_+,r,\\eta$.*\n\n</div>\n\nThe proof of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:step\">3</a> occupies the next three sections. Membership of the output in $\\mathcal U$ is ensured by imposing an additional sufficiently small tolerance around the fixed input pair. The conclusion permits arbitrarily small finite-norm tolerances; it makes no claim of a uniform frequency threshold as those tolerances or the collar width vary. The common exponent $\\gamma$ is the feature that allows a smooth limiting metric to retain a fixed power of excess nodal growth.\n\n\n### Envelopes with a fixed power gain\n\nWe construct envelopes whose gradient integral grows geometrically under iteration. The part of each parent cube available for the next iteration will have more gradient integral than the entire parent had before the change. All scales and gain factors are fixed before any finite approximation order is prescribed.\n\nThroughout this Section, tensor norms in Hessian inequalities are operator norms for $G$. Coordinate derivatives and integrals refer to fixed smooth coordinates. The notation $p,Q,J_h$ is that of <a href=\"#eq:hessian-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hessian-test\">[eq:hessian-test]</a>.\n\n\n#### A seed and the preparation condition\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:seed\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n**Lemma 4**. *There are a number $a_*>0$, a smooth real function $h_0$ on $B$, a $C^1$ neighborhood $\\mathcal G$ of $g_0$ among smooth metrics, and a coordinate chart $\\Omega\\subset\\{t>1/2\\}$ with open sets $U_0\\Subset V\\Subset\\Omega$ such that the following assertions hold uniformly for $G\\in\\mathcal G$:*\n\n1.  *$h_0=-a_*t$ on $\\{t\\le1/2\\}$;*\n\n2.  *$\\max_e J_{h_0}(e)\\ge c_0>0$ on $\\{t\\ge1/4\\}$;*\n\n3.  *on $U_0$, the Hessian of $h_0$ is positive definite with a uniform positive lower bound, and $0<s_{\\min}\\le|\\nabla_Gh_0|_G\\le s_{\\max}$.*\n\n*The maximum in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> is over all admissible tests, including all unit vectors when $\\mathrm dh_0=0$.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-lem-seed\"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-seed</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* Begin with $\\widetilde h=-a_*t$. Direct differentiation on the round sphere gives $$\\label{eq:seed-identities}\n |\\nabla_{g_0}\\widetilde h|_{g_0}^2\n =4a_*^2t(1-t),\\qquad\n \\nabla_{g_0}^2\\widetilde h\n =2a_*(tg_0-\\mathrm dy\\cdot\\mathrm dy).$$ At every point there is a nonzero tangent vector in $\\ker\\mathrm dy$, because $\\mathrm dy:T_xB\\to\\mathbb R^3$ has a four-dimensional domain. A round unit vector $e$ in this kernel satisfies $\\mathrm d\\widetilde h(e)=0$ and $(\\nabla_{g_0}^2\\widetilde h)(e,e)=2a_*t$. Since the Hessian in <a href=\"#eq:seed-identities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:seed-identities\">[eq:seed-identities]</a> has norm at most $2a_*$ and the squared gradient has norm at most $a_*^2$, on $t\\ge1/4$ we obtain $$J_{\\widetilde h}(e)\\ge a_*/2-2a_*^3\\ge a_*/4$$ after choosing $a_*^2\\le1/8$.\n\nAt a point $x_*$ with $t=1$, the Hessian has eigenvalues $2a_*,2a_*,0,0$, and the gradient is zero. On a small neighborhood of $x_*$ contained in $\\{t>1/2\\}$, two Hessian eigenvalues therefore have a uniform positive lower bound and the gradient is arbitrarily small. In round normal coordinates $\\xi$ at $x_*$, add $\\varepsilon|\\xi|^2/2$ times a fixed smooth cutoff equal to one near $\\xi=0$. Choose $\\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small that this is a $C^2$-small perturbation on its support. The resulting function $h_0$ has positive definite Hessian at $x_*$.\n\nHere is why the cutoff region retains the required test. Shrinking its support and then the perturbation if necessary, the perturbed Hessian has two eigenvalues at least $c a_*$, its norm is at most $C a_*$, and $|\\nabla_{g_0}h_0|$ is sufficiently small that $C a_*|\\nabla_{g_0}h_0|^2<c a_*/2$. The positive two-plane intersects $\\ker\\mathrm dh_0$ in dimension at least one. A unit vector in that intersection gives $J_{h_0}\\ge c a_*/2$. This argument also applies at a critical point. Outside the perturbation support the previous $\\ker\\mathrm dy$ test applies.\n\nThese conclusions persist in a sufficiently small common $C^1$ neighborhood of $g_0$. Indeed, the difference of the two Hessians of a fixed function is the connection difference contracted with its differential, and all metric and gradient comparisons use only $C^1$ bounds. Outside the perturbation support, the condition $\\mathrm dy(e)=0$ still implies $\\mathrm dh_0(e)=0$ independently of the metric. On the support, the positive two-plane and small-gradient estimates just established are uniform under a small perturbation.\n\nFinally, positive definiteness at $x_*$ holds on a neighborhood of that point. There is a nearby point with nonzero differential: otherwise $h_0$ would be constant on that neighborhood. Choose a relatively compact neighborhood $U_0$ of such a point where the Hessian remains positive definite and the gradient stays separated from zero. Choose $U_0\\Subset V\\Subset\\Omega\\subset\\{t>1/2\\}$ in a fixed coordinate chart, and shrink $\\mathcal G$ once more. All the stated constants can then be chosen uniformly. Notice that $U_0$ is chosen away from the critical point $x_*$. ◻\n\n</details>\n\n\nWe use the coordinates of $\\Omega$ for all corrugations. They may be taken on a neighborhood of its closure, so metric and inverse metric have common ellipticity and $C^1$ bounds there. Fix positive constants $C_{\\mathrm p}$ and $c_{\\mathrm p}$. A smooth function $h$ is *prepared at scale $S$* on a cube $P$ of side $\\nu S$, $0<S\\le1$, if $$\\label{eq:preparation}\n \\begin{gathered}\n |\\nabla h|>0,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^2h|\\le C_{\\mathrm p}|\\nabla h|/S,\\\\\n \\text{at each point there is a two-plane }\n E\\subset\\ker\\mathrm dh\\text{ such that }\n J_h(e)\\ge c_{\\mathrm p}|\\nabla h|/S\n \\quad(e\\in E,\\ |e|=1).\n \\end{gathered}$$ The plane $E$ need not vary continuously. Cubes are parallel to the fixed coordinate axes, and the inequalities are required on their closures.\n\nThe two Hessian conditions serve different parts of the next corrugation. The Hessian norm bound controls the inherited Hessian and the variation of the parent gradient. When the gradient changes by a small relative amount, we can rotate the favorable two-plane slightly into the hyperplane perpendicular to the new gradient. One further linear tangency constraint then leaves a direction with a positive test. On the region selected for repetition, the modification supplies two favorable directions again. This is why the preparation condition asks for a two-plane even though the envelope ultimately needs only one positive test at each point.\n\nBy Lemma <a href=\"#lem:seed\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:seed\">4</a>, for all sufficiently large $C_{\\mathrm p}$ and sufficiently small $c_{\\mathrm p}>0$, every sufficiently small cube in $U_0$ is prepared for $h_0$ at scale $1$, uniformly over $G\\in\\mathcal G$.\n\n\n#### A repeatable local corrugation\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:corrugation\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n**Lemma 5**. *Under fixed ellipticity and $C^1$ metric bounds in the coordinate patch, there are constants $C_{\\mathrm p}^*>0$, $c_{\\mathrm p}^*>0$ and $\\zeta>0$ with the following property. For any $C_{\\mathrm p}\\ge C_{\\mathrm p}^*$ and $0<c_{\\mathrm p}\\le c_{\\mathrm p}^*$, one may choose a sufficiently small reciprocal integer $\\nu>0$, once and for all, such that every prepared cube $P$ at scale $S\\le1$ admits a smooth modification $v=h+w$, with $w$ supported in the interior of $P$, satisfying:*\n\n1.  *for fixed constants $0<\\alpha\\le1\\le\\beta$ and $c>0$, $$\\alpha|\\nabla h|\\le|\\nabla v|\\le\\beta|\\nabla h|,\n      \\qquad\n      \\max_eJ_v(e)\\ge c|\\nabla h|/S\n      \\quad\\hbox{on }P;$$*\n\n\n2.  *writing $\\ell=\\nu^2S$, some cubes in the partition of $P$ into cubes of side $\\nu\\ell$ are prepared for $v$ at scale $\\ell$ with the same constants in <a href=\"#eq:preparation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:preparation\">[eq:preparation]</a>, and their union $\\mathcal R_P$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:local-mass-gain}\n      \\int_{\\mathcal R_P}|\\nabla v|\\,\\mathrm dx\n      \\ge(1+\\zeta)\\int_P|\\nabla h|\\,\\mathrm dx;$$*\n\n\n3.  *if $s=|\\nabla h(x_P)|$ at the center $x_P$ of $P$, then for every integer $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:corrugation-derivatives}\n      \\|D^jw\\|_{L^\\infty(P)}\\le C_j s\\ell^{1-j}.$$*\n\n*All constants are uniform over prepared functions and cubes and over the stated class of metrics. They may depend on the fixed pattern, preparation constants, and final choice of $\\nu$. No derivative of the input $h$ beyond its prepared second-derivative bound is used. The pattern and $\\zeta$ are chosen before the preparation constants and the final $\\nu$; increasing $C_{\\mathrm p}$ or decreasing $c_{\\mathrm p}$ only requires taking $\\nu$ smaller.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-lem-corrugation\"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-corrugation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The pattern will depend on three variables transverse to the parent gradient frozen at its center. Radial wells in these variables have two positive tangential Hessian directions; a small amplitude controls their potentially negative radial contribution to $J_v$. We first choose the wells and smaller concentric balls that will supply the repeatable gain. We then place this pattern in the parent and verify the Hessian test, including where the parent cutoff varies.\n\n\n###### The periodic wells.\n\n\n\nChoose a smooth function $F$ on $[0,1]$, positive on $(0,1)$, equal to $v$ near $v=0$, and flat at $v=1$. Inside a unit cube in $\\mathbb R^3$ take finitely many balls with pairwise disjoint closures and radii $R_i>0$. On a ball, at radius $r$ from its center, set $$\\psi(r)=-\\int_r^{R_i}F(q/R_i)\\,\\mathrm dq,\\qquad\n f(r)=F(r/R_i),\\qquad u_0(r)=f(r)/r.$$ Extend $\\psi$ by zero outside the balls and periodically to $\\mathbb R^3$. It is smooth: near a center it is a constant plus $r^2/(2R_i)$, and it is flat at the outer boundary. The extended value of $u_0$ at the center is $1/R_i$. Write $\\sigma$ for the radial unit vector. Then $$\\label{eq:well-identities}\n \\nabla\\psi=f\\sigma,\\qquad\n D^2\\psi=f'\\sigma\\otimes\\sigma\n              +u_0(I-\\sigma\\otimes\\sigma),\\qquad\n f^2|f'|\\le C u_0,\\quad f^2\\le C u_0.$$ The first constant in these inequalities is independent of the radii, because, for $v=r/R_i$, $$\\frac{f^2|f'|}{u_0}=vF(v)|F'(v)|.$$ The second follows from $f^2/u_0=R_i vF(v)$ and the bounded radii. Set $f,f',u_0$ equal to zero outside the balls. These coefficients are continuous, and $$\\label{eq:well-vanishing}\n u_0=0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad f=f'=\\psi=0.$$ At a center $f'=u_0$, so any choice of $\\sigma$ gives the same Hessian in <a href=\"#eq:well-identities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:well-identities\">[eq:well-identities]</a>; outside the balls choose $\\sigma$ arbitrarily as well.\n\nChoose a small amplitude $a>0$. Its smallness will use only $F$ and the radius-independent first bound in <a href=\"#eq:well-identities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:well-identities\">[eq:well-identities]</a>. In particular it can be chosen before the packing. The mean gradient factor on any one well is $$\\mathcal W(a)=3\\int_0^1v^2\\sqrt{1+a^2F(v)^2}\\,\\mathrm dv>1.$$ Put $\\eta_0=(\\mathcal W(a)-1)/8$. Choose the finite packing with volume fraction $\\varpi$ such that $$\\varpi\\mathcal W(a)>1+6\\eta_0.$$ Such a packing exists. To see this without requiring an infinite packing in the construction, fill a fixed positive fraction of any remaining open set by finitely many closed grid cubes lying inside it, and put a ball with strictly smaller radius than the inradius in each cube. This captures a fixed positive fraction of the remaining volume. Finitely many repetitions make the uncovered volume as small as desired, with all ball closures still disjoint.\n\n\nChoose $\\eta>0$ so small that, on the core balls $r<(1-\\eta)R_i$, the weighted integral per period satisfies $$C_{\\mathrm{core}}\n :=\\sum_i\\int_{r<(1-\\eta)R_i}\\sqrt{1+a^2f^2}\\,\\mathrm dz'\n >\\varpi\\mathcal W(a)-\\eta_0>1+5\\eta_0.$$ Choose an inner cube fraction $q\\in(0,1)$ such that $$\\label{eq:repeat-slack}\n qC_{\\mathrm{core}}>C_{\\mathrm{core}}-\\eta_0>1+4\\eta_0.$$ Here $q$ is a four-dimensional volume fraction. Fix a smooth cutoff on the unit parent cube which equals one on the concentric inner cube of fraction $q$, is supported in the parent interior, and is flat wherever it is zero. Products of one-dimensional plateau cutoffs give such a function. The wells, cores, and cutoff are now fixed. All their derivative bounds are finite, although no bound uniform in derivative order is asserted.\n\n\n###### Normalization on a prepared parent.\n\n\nTake affine coordinates $z=(z_1,z')$ centered at $x_P$, making the metric Euclidean at the center and $\\nabla h(x_P)=s\\partial_{z_1}$. The linear maps and their inverses have uniform bounds; the image of $P$ is a parallelepiped of uniformly bounded distortion. The coordinate identity relating $D(\\mathrm dh)$ to the covariant Hessian and the bounded connection gives $$|D(\\mathrm dh)|\\le C|\\mathrm dh|/S$$ on $P$, since $S\\le1$. Integration along segments of length $O(\\nu S)$ yields relative upper and lower bounds for the gradient and then $$\\label{eq:parent-normalization}\n \\mathrm dh/s=\\mathrm dz_1+O(\\nu),\\qquad\n (G_{ij})_z=I+O(\\nu),\\qquad\n |\\nabla h|/s=1+O(\\nu).$$ The constants here may depend on the preparation constants. Only the bounds already specified in the lemma enter them.\n\n\n\nRescale the fixed parent cutoff to $P$ and call it $\\chi$. Set $$\\label{eq:corrugation-formula}\n v=h+s\\ell a\\chi\\psi(z'/\\ell),\\qquad \\ell=\\nu^2S.$$ In the following estimates the radial coefficients are evaluated at $z'/\\ell$. A cutoff derivative at its parent scale is denoted $D_{\\mathrm{sc}}\\chi$; thus an ordinary derivative of $\\chi$ is $(\\nu S)^{-1}D_{\\mathrm{sc}}\\chi$. The bounded affine changes only alter the fixed bounds for these scaled derivatives. Since $\\ell/(\\nu S)=\\nu$, Equation <a href=\"#eq:corrugation-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:corrugation-formula\">[eq:corrugation-formula]</a> gives $$\\label{eq:new-gradient}\n \\mathrm dv/s=\\mathrm dz_1+a\\chi f\\sigma\\cdot\\mathrm dz'+O(\\nu).$$ This proves the gradient comparison in <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> for fixed $\\alpha,\\beta$, when $\\nu$ is sufficiently small.\n\n\n###### The new Hessian contribution.\n\n\nWrite $$D=\\frac{\\nabla v}{\\sqrt{1+|\\nabla v|^2}},\\qquad\n \\mathcal F=\\{e:\\mathrm dv(e)=0,\\ \\sigma\\cdot e'=0\\},$$ where $e'$ denotes the static transverse components in the affine coordinates. The two defining linear conditions are independent by <a href=\"#eq:new-gradient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:new-gradient\">[eq:new-gradient]</a>, so $\\mathcal F$ is two-dimensional. For a metric unit vector $e\\in\\mathcal F$, $$\\label{eq:test-components}\n |e'|^2\\ge\\tfrac12,\\qquad\n |\\sigma\\cdot D'|\\le C_0a\\chi f+C\\nu.$$ Indeed $e_1=O(\\nu)$ follows from the two linear conditions. The second estimate follows from <a href=\"#eq:new-gradient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:new-gradient\">[eq:new-gradient]</a> and $G^{ij}=\\delta_{ij}+O(\\nu)$, using a denominator at least a fixed multiple of $s$. We may take $C_0$ absolute by first taking $\\nu$ small for the fixed data. These arguments are uniform even when $s$ is arbitrarily small or large.\n\nLet $\\Pi_\\sigma$ be Euclidean projection onto $\\sigma^\\perp$ in the transverse space. The leading contribution of $v-h$ to its Hessian test on $D,e$, divided by $s/\\ell$, is $$a\\chi\\left[\n f'(\\sigma\\cdot D')^2+\n u_0\\bigl(|\\Pi_\\sigma D'|^2+|e'|^2\\bigr)\\right].$$ By <a href=\"#eq:test-components\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:test-components\">[eq:test-components]</a>, this is at least $$a\\chi\\left[\n \\tfrac12u_0-|f'|\\bigl(2C_0^2a^2\\chi^2 f^2+C\\nu^2\\bigr)\n \\right].$$ At the initial choice of amplitude, impose $2C_0^2a^2\\sup_v vF(v)|F'(v)|\\le1/4$. The main term is then at least $a\\chi u_0/4-Ca\\nu^2\\chi|f'|$.\n\nThe mixed cutoff terms on $e,e$ vanish because $\\sigma\\cdot e'=0$. On $D,D$ their size is bounded by $$C\\nu f|D_{\\mathrm{sc}}\\chi|(\\chi f+\\nu).$$ The pure cutoff terms have size at most $C\\nu^2|\\psi||D_{\\mathrm{sc}}^2\\chi|$. Finally, replacing the coordinate Hessian of $v-h$ by its covariant Hessian gives an error of size $$C\\ell(\\chi f+\\nu)$$ in the same units, because the connection is uniformly bounded.\n\n\nWe verify uniformity of these errors also where the favorable coefficient vanishes. Set $X=\\chi u_0$. On a fixed compact parameter space, if continuous functions $X,b\\ge0$ satisfy $b=0$ on $\\{X=0\\}$, then $$\\label{eq:vanishing-coefficient}\n \\sup\\frac{\\nu^2b}{X+\\nu^2}\\longrightarrow0\n \\qquad(\\nu\\longrightarrow0).$$ For $X\\ge\\tau$ the quotient is at most $\\nu^2\\sup b/\\tau$; for $X<\\tau$ it is at most $\\sup_{X<\\tau}b$, which tends to zero as $\\tau\\downarrow0$. The compact parameter space here consists of cutoff position, period position, and bounded affine frames. Taking its closure is harmless. Transformed cutoff derivatives are continuous on this space and have uniform bounds.\n\n\nApply <a href=\"#eq:vanishing-coefficient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:vanishing-coefficient\">[eq:vanishing-coefficient]</a> to $b=\\chi|f'|$, $b=f|D_{\\mathrm{sc}}\\chi|$, and $b=|\\psi||D_{\\mathrm{sc}}^2\\chi|$. Their vanishing follows from <a href=\"#eq:well-vanishing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:well-vanishing\">[eq:well-vanishing]</a> and the flatness of $\\chi$ on its zero set. The other mixed term satisfies $\\nu\\chi f^2|D_{\\mathrm{sc}}\\chi|\\le C\\nu X$. Moreover, $\\chi f\\le C\\sqrt X$ and $\\ell\\le\\nu^2$ give $$\\frac{\\ell(\\chi f+\\nu)}{X+\\nu^2}\n \\le C\\frac{\\nu^2\\sqrt X}{X+\\nu^2}+\\nu\n \\le C'\\nu.$$ We have proved, uniformly in all prepared parents and allowed tests, $$\\label{eq:corrugation-hessian-error}\n \\frac{\\ell}{s}\\left[\n  \\nabla^2(v-h)(D,D)+\\nabla^2(v-h)(e,e)\\right]\n \\ge c_a X-\\varepsilon(\\nu)(X+\\nu^2),\n \\qquad \\varepsilon(\\nu)\\longrightarrow0,$$ for a fixed $c_a>0$. No positive lower bound on a nonzero value of $\\chi$ or $u_0$ has been used.\n\n\n###### Retaining one direction everywhere.\n\nThe old Hessian has norm $O(\\nu^2)$ after division by $s/\\ell$. Thus the preceding estimate compares a favorable new contribution of size $X$ with inherited terms of size $\\nu^2$. When $X$ dominates $\\nu^2$, the new favorable directions suffice. When $X=O(\\nu^2)$, the gradients are close enough to carry an old favorable direction to the new tangent space.
